TECHNICAL DISCLOSURE DRAFT · nt:coupled-coils

Coupled-coil energy and phase bench

Patent-style technical disclosure draft; unresolved source and filing requirements remain explicit

The separate numbered drawing sheets use an official-rule-informed mechanical profile. This explanatory document does not certify a patent application, physical performance or construction readiness.

Technical field

Coupled-coil energy and phase bench: electricity / magnetic coupling. An adjustable low-voltage, two-loop bench with complete energy accounting and simultaneous phase-resolved readout.

Background and source boundary

The R1/R2 lumped circuit is a numerical fixture. Pack dimensions do not derive L, M, resistance, capacitance or sensor performance.

Numerical fixture equations in linked frozen contracts; layout geometry does not determine circuit parameters.

Editorial relevance mapping, not an upstream claim that the apparatus validates these proposals.

Summary of the documented proposal

Track source loading, receiver response and the complete signed energy account.

The source identities, stipulated model values and measured quantities are separate. Proposed improvements below are not silently substituted into the source apparatus.

Brief description of drawings

FIG. 1: source-supported plan documentation of Coupled-coil energy and phase bench. Reference numerals identify documented components; the sheet does not supply missing manufacturing tolerances or ratings.

FIG. 2: source-supported elevation documentation of Coupled-coil energy and phase bench. Reference numerals identify documented components; the sheet does not supply missing manufacturing tolerances or ratings.

FIG. 3: source-supported flow documentation of Coupled-coil energy and phase bench. Reference numerals identify documented components; the sheet does not supply missing manufacturing tolerances or ratings.

Detailed description and reference signs

100: Isolated source; source component source.

102: Primary winding resistance R₁; source component R1.

104: Primary capacitance C₁; source component C1.

120: Primary coil L₁; source component L1.

122: Receiving coil L₂; source component L2.

106: Receiver capacitance C₂; source component C2.

108: Receiver winding resistance R₂; source component R2.

110: Useful load resistance Rᴸ; source component RL.

112: Isolated coherent readout; source component recorder.

120: Primary coil; partial conceptual circuit relationship, not complete wiring; source component L1.

110: Useful load resistance; source component RL.

Start with the pinned model

Open the source record and attached contract; write down the observed quantity, units and rival explanation.

Run the existing numerical controls before selecting physical parts; record the exact source commit and original parameter conventions.

Close the specification gap

Transcribe the pinned two-loop topology and winding-dot convention into a reviewed connection drawing.

List actual coil, capacitor, load, source and probe part numbers; measure L, M and R before comparing hardware with the numerical contract.

Commission ordinary controls before a claimed gain

With the source at zero, record background and instrument offsets.

Use a known dummy load and minimum documented source setting; acquire all channels on the same reference.

Change receiving load and coupling one variable at a time; retain raw traces and endpoints for storage.

Working examples and experimental status

Status: proposed/source-defined; no physical commissioning or measured performance is asserted by this dossier.

Source off; uncoupled coils; known load; reversed winding sign; repeated gap settings.

Independent signed-work quadrature and endpoint storage; peak/RMS check.

Claims-support worksheet

The reference-sign table and source-bound feature descriptions support review of a future claim set. No novelty, inventive-step, ownership, freedom-to-operate or patent-grant conclusion has been made. No submitted claims or applicant details have been invented.

Enablement/sufficiency remains blocked wherever essential materials, ratings, calibration, reproducible methods or source definitions are missing.

Purpose and practical applicability

Track source loading, receiver response and the complete signed energy account.

This is an experimental documentation method or proposed measurement arrangement, not proof of energy gain or a certified construction plan.

Setup from scratch

  • Understand peak versusRMS, common phase reference and passive energy storage.
  • Use an isolated low-voltage source only after actual component/probe limits are documented.

Start with the pinned model

  • Open the source record and attached contract; write down the observed quantity, units and rival explanation.
  • Run the existing numerical controls before selecting physical parts; record the exact source commit and original parameter conventions.

Checks

  • The source record remains proposed/unbuilt.
  • A completed software test is not a completed apparatus test.

Stop conditions

  • The contract or source identity is missing, or its peak/RMS and unit convention is unresolved.

Close the specification gap

  • Transcribe the pinned two-loop topology and winding-dot convention into a reviewed connection drawing.
  • List actual coil, capacitor, load, source and probe part numbers; measure L, M and R before comparing hardware with the numerical contract.

Checks

  • Record turn count, wire, core and ratings as chosen implementation values, not inherited facts.

Stop conditions

  • Any rating, ground path, isolation requirement or actual inductance is unknown.

Commission ordinary controls before a claimed gain

  • With the source at zero, record background and instrument offsets.
  • Use a known dummy load and minimum documented source setting; acquire all channels on the same reference.
  • Change receiving load and coupling one variable at a time; retain raw traces and endpoints for storage.

Checks

  • Source-off yields the declared null within measured uncertainty.
  • Uncoupled receiver and sign-reversedM controls match the implemented model.

Stop conditions

  • Clipping, heating, drift,ground coupling or missing source/storage work prevents a closed account.

Parts-selection worksheet

Drive and simultaneous phase-aware readout

One source; enough coherent channels for all observables · Source voltage, primary current, receiver current and load voltage; bandwidth and phase error

  • Select differential or isolated channels with a common timebase.
  • Characterize source impedance, channel phase and the burden added by every probe. A two-input audio interface alone is not the required four-observable electrical readout.

Search terms: isolated low-voltage waveform generator; coherent differential four-channel acquisition

Two adjustable coils

Two coils · Self-inductance L1/L2, mutual inductance M, resistance and geometry

  • Frozen R1 declares 10 mH and 40 mH, with M = 3 mH. Geometry does not supply a winding turn count.
  • Measure signed mutual inductance and both self-inductances at every gap; choose wire, core, insulation and thermal ratings separately.

Search terms: coupled inductors; measured mutual inductance; winding resistance

Resonating capacitances

Two capacitors · C1/C2, AC voltage, ripple current and tolerance

  • The R1 values 10 µF and 2.5 µF are numerical targets, not verified MKP10 stock codes.
  • Inspect the chosen part’s AC, ripple, tolerance and temperature specifications.

Search terms: polypropylene pulse capacitor; 10 uF 2.5 uF AC rating

WIMA MKP10 film-capacitor family · role reference, not a validated drop-in or commissioned system · availability unverified; price unverified

Current readout and useful load

Two current-sense roles and one useful load · Resistance, power rating and temperature coefficient

  • Choose shunts so their burden is included in the circuit model.
  • Recalculate dissipation and bandwidth. A WSL current-sense part is not a recommendation for the 20 Ω useful load.

Search terms: four-terminal current shunt; noninductive load resistor power rating

Vishay WSL current-sense resistor family · role reference, not a validated drop-in or commissioned system · availability unverified; price unverified

Commissioning and falsification

  • Source off; uncoupled coils; known load; reversed winding sign; repeated gap settings.
  • Independent signed-work quadrature and endpoint storage; peak/RMS check.
  • The passive forward model predicts held-out load changes within a predeclared uncertainty.
  • Measure every energy term or identify it explicitly as modeled; report residuals with uncertainty.
  • A repeatable budget failure beyond uncertainty rejects the present implementation or measurement account.
  • Voltage gain alone cannot establish unexplained energy or identify its source.

symbol		Coupling describes how strongly two modeled coils interact.

In this passive positive-definite two-coil fixture, M = k√(L1 L2) and |k| < 1.

k

dimensionless

A selected k is not a measured coil calibration.

symbol		Resistance converts electrical energy to heat.

The receiving load and winding/internal losses are accounted for separately.

Ri

ohm (Ω)

R1 can also name a research round; ℝ denotes real numbers, not resistance.

defined

terminology.electricity.resistance
q
symbol		Charge measures how much electricity has accumulated.

q is an instantaneous scalar or vector; its time derivative is current.

q

coulomb (C)

The unit C means coulomb here, not capacitance.

defined

terminology.electricity.charge
i
symbol		Current is the flow rate of electric charge.

Lowercase i(t) is instantaneous; uppercase I is a phasor whose peak/RMS convention must be declared.

i

ampere (A)

The imaginary unit i in an impedance equation is a different meaning.

defined

terminology.electricity.work
RMS
abbreviation		RMS is the square root of the mean squared signal.

For a pure sine only, peak amplitude is √2 times RMS. For a fixed resistor, RMS determines average heating.

RMS

same unit as the signal

RMS is not the arithmetic mean or a universal peak conversion.

defined

contracts[1].record.model.coherent_reconstruction
δ
symbol		Marks over a symbol tell you which version, change or bound is meant.

A hat denotes an estimate; δ often a difference or perturbation; Δ can denote a final-minus-initial change or a supplied error bound, according to the contract; a dot is a time derivative.

δ

depends on quantity; derivative adds /s

Δa and ΔM in these contracts are error-bound radii, not temporal changes. Every symbol needs declared units; ż is velocity, not position.

sine-amplitude
source-clause		For a sine across a resistor, 1 V peak gives half the heating power of 1 V RMS at the same resistance.

Vpeak = √2 Vrms; P_R = Vrms²/R = Vpeak²/(2R)

Vpeak = √2 Vrms; P_R = Vrms²/R = Vpeak²/(2R)

V, Ω, W; at R=1 Ω: 1 V peak →0.5 W, 1 V RMS →1 W.

Pure sinusoid; ideal fixed resistor; upstream phasors peak, host circuit input RMS.

source-clause		Source work goes to useful load, heating losses and a change of stored energy.

Win = Wload + Wloss + ΔE; E = ½ iᵀLi + ½ qᵀdiag(1/C)q

Win = Wload + Wloss + ΔE; E = ½ iᵀLi + ½ qᵀdiag(1/C)q

Each work/storage term J; i A, q C, L H, capacitance F.

Instantaneous states and signed finite-time work; no RMS substitution into instantaneous storage.

peak-phasor-power
source-clause		The factor one-half converts peak sinusoidal amplitudes into cycle-average power.

Psource = ½ Re(V conjugate(I1)); Pload = ½ RL |I2|²

V×A = W; Ω×A² = W.

paper-wpt-2025

evidence.paper-wpt-2025

Research-led proposed improvements

Compare coupling and load robustness before adaptive control

proposed research improvement; not implemented or physically validated: Record a calibrated baseline across coil placement and receiving load. Evaluate an adaptive-load candidate as a separate design branch with its converter and controller power inside the energy boundary.

The 2025 primary paper makes source gain and receiver loss explicit; its active PT model motivates a controlled comparison rather than silently modifying the passive source fixture.

  • Keep source R1/R2 peak-voltage values unchanged in the baseline.
  • Characterize actual self/mutual inductance and phase-aware sensing at each geometry.
  • Include every controller/drive/auxiliary supply, load heat, winding loss and endpoint storage in the energy account.
  • Predefine comparable operating points, uncertainties and pass/fail criteria before a new hardware branch.

Sources: paper-wpt-2025

Log frequency, supply and tuning state as separate control variables

proposed research improvement; not implemented or physically validated: Define a separate controlled-excitation test branch whose experimental log records drive frequency, supply voltage, selected capacitor configuration and source power at each step.

A recent 2026 application distinguishes these control variables. Keeping them separately recorded prevents an apparent improvement caused by an unnoticed input-power change.

  • Preserve the frozen source baseline and proposed branch as different revisions.
  • Document all switched capacitance ratings, transient limits and controller/converter auxiliary power before assembly.
  • Run fixed-load and fixed-source controls with calibrated simultaneous voltage/current acquisition.
  • Compare total energy and uncertainty, not receiver voltage alone.

Sources: US20260246304A1

Separate input, branch and load energy measurement boundaries

proposed research improvement; not implemented or physically validated: Create a commissioning worksheet with independently identified input, transfer-branch and receiving-load energy channels, signed polarity and start/end storage states.

The 2026 application explicitly distinguishes input and distributed energy accounting. The transferable research question is boundary completeness; its road-metering implementation is not adopted.

  • Calibrate each voltage/current channel including phase and clock alignment.
  • Include internal loss, external supports/auxiliary sources and endpoint stored energy.
  • Compare an independent energy quadrature with the original solver accounting.
  • Do not equate current allocation with measured load energy without the corresponding voltage and phase.

Sources: US20260233630A1

Test load-state transition timing and feedback delay

proposed research improvement; not implemented or physically validated: Preregister a test for whether load changes, acquisition timing or delayed feedback can create apparent power-gain peaks; keep fixed-state baselines and state-transition receipts.

The 2026 application identifies load states, coupling references and synchronization. They motivate controls for transient artifacts before any adaptive control is added.

  • Log the exact source, load and feedback state against a shared clock.
  • Compare delayed and exchanged-channel controls.
  • Measure rather than infer coupling from gap or a product name.
  • Record every drive/controller supply and validate stability before a hardware fork.

Sources: US20260229927A1

Standards and sufficiency checklist

Drawing-sheet mechanics · checked

All generated sheets pass measured A4, margin, monochrome, actual reference-ink-height and leader/reference-clearance gates. These checks cover the stated common-profile mechanics only.

Reference signs and description · checked

All 9 generated component reference numerals resolve consistently to a source component and description across views.

Notation and scientific values · blocked

1000/1266 register entries are defined. All unresolved entries remain explicitly visible; no source value is silently repaired.

Reproducible setup and sufficiency · blocked

desk study ready; apparatus conditional—not released for construction

Application-specific filing and patentability · requires-review

Applicant/inventor details, actual claim scope/support, novelty, inventive step, ownership, added matter and receiving-office/e-filing requirements require a specific application review. No universal EU patent standard is asserted; EPO requirements are identified by authority and date.

Unresolved specifications and definitions

  • Unbuilt layout, no experimental observation or detector sensitivity.
  • Turns, wire gauge, core, parasitics and actual L/M remain unspecified; this is not a validated construction kit.
  • An inferred effective receiver-port source is not a physical-cause identification.
  • Optional receiver injection is a deliberate positive control whose work must be included.
  • Coil turns, core, wire, actual L/M, component ratings, source impedance and calibrated four-observable readout remain unspecified.
  • 266 notation/source-token entries still need a source-grounded definition; consult the explicit register.

Reviewed sources and reading depth

USPTO MPEP §608.02 — Drawing · Selected drawing/completeness/correction passages read from the official §608 page; not all 874 kB.

Necessary drawings and consistent disclosure matter; later drawings cannot cure original insufficient disclosure or add original claim scope. MPEP guidance is distinct from the current regulation; displayed subsection revision is January 2024.

37 CFR §1.84 — Standards for drawings · Current eCFR title displayed as of 13 November 2026. Selected drawing standards read.

A4 or US Letter, specified margins, black-line default, ≥3.2 mm characters, consistent references and US figure/sheet rules. Current regulation text retrieved; not an audit of every filing channel, exception or application type.

EPC Rule46 — deleted historical form-of-drawings provision · Current official page and deletion/version-history note read; historical text distinguished from current law.

Deleted effective 1 February 2023 by CA/D10/22; do not cite former Rule46 as the current drawing authority. Historical text remains displayed on the official page and can mislead a title-only reading.

EPC Rule42 — content of description · Full short operative description provision read.

Technical field, background art, problem/solution, figures, at least one detailed way of carrying out, industrial applicability where not obvious. A description template cannot supply absent enabling parameters or establish a claimed technical effect.

EPC Rule43 — form and content of claims · Selected technical-feature, dependent-claim, numbering and reference-sign provisions read.

Claims define technical features; number claims; avoid reliance on figures alone; parenthesised reference signs may aid intelligibility without limiting the claim. No legal claim drafting or novelty/inventive-step conclusion for the repositories is provided.

EPC Article83 — disclosure of invention · Full short operative disclosure provision read.

Disclose sufficiently clearly and completely for a person skilled in the art to carry out the invention. A neat drawing does not satisfy sufficiency when necessary operating information is missing.

EPO Guidelines2026 F-II4.1 — general description remarks · Selected purpose-of-description paragraphs read.

Description enables practice and understanding of the contribution to the art. Guidance on examination, not a substitute for the EPC or case-specific legal assessment.

PCT Rule11 — physical requirements of international application · Selected sheet/margin/numbering/text/drawing provisions read.

A4, drawing margins, minimal words, black-line drawings, ≥3.2mm drawing characters and consistent reference signs. PCT international-phase rules differ from EPO regional electronic practice; request-text2.1mm and ordinary-text2.8mm are not drawing-glyph minima.

EPC Rule49(2) — presentation requirements delegated to EPO President · Selected current paragraph and references read; tracked/deleted prior paragraphs not treated as current authority.

The President determines presentation requirements; page links OJ2025A49 and A57. Current and struck/deleted historical wording coexist in the page; use the current President decision for mechanical details.

EPO President decision25November2022 — OJ2022A113 · Selected drawing/presentation provisions and successor supersession text read.

Former delegated presentation requirements, superseded on 1 October 2025 by OJ2025A49. Not the current EPO drawing authority.

EPO Guidelines2026 A-IX3 — paper-drawing conditions · Selected paper/reproduction paragraphs and A49 cross-reference read.

Paper conditions remain relevant to paper filings; the page updates its decision reference to2025A49. Not used as an electronic-colour prohibition; current A49 controls that distinction.

EPO Guidelines2026 F-III1 — sufficiency · Selected essential-feature, whole-area-claimed and structure/function discussion read.

Essential features need sufficient detail; broad claims need enabling coverage without undue burden; structure and function must be explained. A beginner tutorial is an educational extension; the legal addressee is the person skilled in the art.

EPC Article84 — claims · Full short operative claims provision read.

Claims define protection sought and must be clear, concise and supported by the description. Model correlations and unexplained performance assertions do not create claim support.

USPTO MPEP §2164 — Enablement requirement · Selected opening enablement discussion and undue-experimentation passages read.

Claims define the invention to enable; sufficient making/using information is required without undue experimentation. No conclusion that a hypothetical setup satisfies 35 USC112(a); later case law beyond passages read was not exhaustively audited.

EPO President decision7July2025 — OJ2025A49 · Operative drawing/presentation articles, supersession and effective-date provisions read.

Effective1October2025: A4, margins, ≥3.2mm drawing characters, minimal text; electronic colour/greyscale permitted subject to contrast and300dpi suitability. Technical profile alignment is not certification, acceptance, sufficiency or a claim of EU-wide harmonised patent law.

EPO notice5September2025 — colour/greyscale drawings, OJ2025A57 · Selected Euro-direct, PCT and Euro-PCT transition paragraphs read.

EPO electronic colour is permitted under A49; PCT international-phase colour remains inadmissible; Euro-PCT transition depends on dates and PATENTSCOPE availability. Do not generalise EPO electronic colour permission to every office, paper filing or PCT international publication.

EPC Article123 — amendments · Selected no-added-subject-matter and post-grant scope provisions read.

An amended European application/patent may not extend beyond the content as filed; post-grant protection may not be extended. An educational later drawing is not a reconstruction of what an earlier patent application disclosed.

37 CFR §1.71 — Detailed description and specification · Current eCFR title as of 13 November 2026; selected description paragraphs read.

Describe making and using in full, clear, concise and exact terms sufficient for a person skilled in the art. Not an assessment that any repository proposal is enabled or patentable.

Maximizing wireless power transfer efficiency at exceptional points (2025) · Selected full-text sections read: Abstract, Results: exceptional point and maximum efficiency; Conclusions; selected coupled-mode model paragraphs

The authors propose adaptive virtual-load loss adjustment in an active parity-time-symmetric WPT system; source gain and receiver losses are explicit. This active converter system is not the existing passive R1/R2 fixture. No efficiency, component value or hardware performance is transferred to this workbench.

Deep room impulse response completion (2025) · Selected full-text sections read: Abstract, Introduction, Discussion, Conclusion and data-availability paragraphs

DECOR predicts a late reverberation tail from an early room impulse response; the authors report degraded performance on an unseen dataset. A predicted tail is synthetic. It cannot recover a unique monument geometry, material response or historical sound without independent measurements.

US20260246304A1 - Systems and Methods for Dynamically Tuning a Wireless Power Transfer System · Abstract and claim1 read; selected controller-description context

The application describes jointly configurable drive frequency, supply voltage and a capacitor bank under transmitter control. Published application text is a proposal, not independent validation. Its controller/converter cannot be substituted into the fixed passive source model or treated as permission to manufacture.

US20260233630A1 - Method and system for metering electricity transmitted to electric vehicles over a wireless power transfer system · Abstract and claims1–5 read; selected field/background definitions

The application separates management-unit energy input and per-transmitter distribution for energy metering. Road/vehicle accounting is a different system boundary; current-only allocation is not sufficient proof of full phase-aware receiver energy in this bench.

US20260229927A1 - Dynamic control of wireless power for a variable load · Abstract and claims1–8 read; selected technical-field context

The application describes reference load states, feedback, coupling-factor references and synchronization of control changes. A communication/control proposal does not establish a calibrated coupling factor, stable closed-loop operation or improved efficiency in this fixture.

US20240029705A1 - Acoustic levitation system, computer-implemented method for levitating an object, computer program and non-volatile data carrier · Abstract read; selected claim1 and description-of-figures context

The application describes phased acoustic transducer elements and a reflector-relative standing-wave arrangement. This is a separate ultrasonic hardware proposal, not an effect produced by phone audio, a monument image or the current audible sonification. No operating power, exposure limit or compatible hardware is established here.

WIMA MKP10 polypropylene pulse capacitors · Product-page construction, capacitance/voltage range and electrical-data overview read

Manufacturer describes polypropylene pulse capacitors and model-dependent capacitance, voltage and lead-spacing ranges. Family range does not verify availability, tolerance or AC/ripple rating of a chosen10µF/2.5µF part. No purchasing recommendation or complete BOM approval.

Vishay WSL Power Metal Strip resistors · document30100 · PDF p1 standard electrical specifications and rating/derating note read; full device design not evaluated

Model-specific low-value resistance, tolerance and power ratings are tabulated; WSL2512 values above0.1Ω have a derating note. A current-sense shunt is not a direct replacement for the frozen2Ω/3Ω winding resistance or20Ω load. Final value/package/thermal design must be selected and measured.

The search for low-mass axion dark matter with ABRACADABRA-10cm ·2021 · Actual arXiv fullHTML: abstract, selected magnet-off-veto discussion and SupplementaryIII Detector calibration read; entire paper not read

End-to-end calibration, known injection and magnet-off/background checks address the complete transfer; the paper reports no significant evidence for axions. This bench borrows calibration logic, not cryogenic apparatus specifications, sensitivity, axion coupling or discovery significance. Their peak-to-peak/RMS conversion is specific to their readout.

Tesla US1119732A · Apparatus for transmitting electrical energy · GooglePatents metadata and selected apparatus-description paragraphs on coupled/tuned circuits read; legal claims not fully analyzed

The patent describes primary/secondary inductive relationships and resonant adjustment. A patent records an asserted design, not tested efficiency, overunity, safety or a construction release. No high-voltage patent apparatus is proposed for this bench.

Maximizing wireless power transfer efficiency at exceptional points (2025) · Selected retained full text read independently: abstract, gain/loss Results and inverter/controller description, Conclusions.

The authors propose adaptive virtual-load loss adjustment in an active parity-time-symmetric WPT system; source gain and receiver losses are explicit. This active converter system is not the existing passive R1/R2 fixture. No efficiency, component value or hardware performance is transferred to this workbench.
